Sunk cost is preserved: Kimi 559 input/1,166 output tokens, 14.402 seconds and USD 0.0051770629; Gemini 561 input/2,188 output tokens, 12 grounding queries, 16.776 seconds and USD 0.1764651; cumulative USD 0.1816421629. Kimi produced one schema-valid plan but it was not used for execution, recorded as `confirmedPlanMismatch`.

v2.0.1 keeps the agreed model-first, multi-turn Kimi intent step and strengthens its instruction to copy explicit country/count/role constraints. Once a user has confirmed a plan, the confirmed structured constraints are authoritative; a later lightweight template-fit call may recommend coverage/template changes but cannot silently change scope. The experiment harness now records divergence as a warning and continues with the confirmed frozen plan. This change does not alter search, evidence, scoring, sample or win gates. It prevents a model fidelity error from wasting all downstream discovery/evidence cost or shrinking the requested output.

### Stage 11: bounded structured-intent retry and provider redundancy v2.0.2

The next CO Retail Product attempt again stopped before search: Kimi returned truncated JSON at exactly the former 2,000-token light-intent ceiling. This added 601 input/2,000 output tokens, 33.940 seconds and USD 0.0085371301 with zero valid or downstream-used output. Cumulative experiment cost is USD 0.1901792930; no Product discovery, evidence, correction or scoring cost exists yet.

The production intent agent now retries invalid/truncated Kimi structured output once, aggregates every attempt's tokens/latency/retry count instead of pricing only the last response, and raises the light ceiling to 4,000 tokens. If Kimi still fails, the previously approved cross-company same-capability redundancy uses DeepSeek Flash and records actual provider/model, fallback state, input/output, latency, attempts and warnings. Deterministic routing remains last-resort degraded UI behavior, not a silent production substitute. Nineteen focused tests cover Kimi success, retry aggregation, provider fallback, failure telemetry and Colombia 50-slot invariants; external calls/cost for implementation verification are 0 and discarded code output is 0.

### Stage 12: Colombia model-provider outage and cache-safe recovery v2.0.3

The first successful CO Retail Product traversal generated 65 raw discovery results, 31 new unique companies, 27 fresh-evidence/correction inputs and 27 deterministic correction outputs, but zero publishable role corrections, score inputs or final outputs. DeepSeek direct inference returned `Insufficient Balance`; because no `LEAD_AI_FALLBACK_*` route was configured, every candidate remained `Unresolved`. Product spent USD 0.7530831701 (search USD 0.222, initial Tavily evidence USD 0.320, correction Tavily evidence USD 0.192, intent/RAG USD 0.0190831701) with zero downstream final utilization. Together with the frozen Gemini result and two earlier intent failures, cumulative experiment spend became USD 0.9295482701. SearchAPI also returned quota HTTP 429, while Brave rejected `country=CO` with HTTP 422.

v2.0.3 makes a configured OpenRouter key an automatic public-only same-tier DeepSeek fallback after bounded direct-provider failure. Packets containing private cooperation-path memory remain `private-workspace` and require a separately approved private fallback. Successful fallback telemetry now carries the requested and actual provider/model, primary plus fallback attempts, token usage and OpenRouter-reported cash cost. Brave uses `ALL` for unsupported country parameters while retaining the country name in its localized query. A minimal live OpenRouter DeepSeek structured-output diagnostic used 87 input and 130 output tokens, returned one valid/used result, cost USD 0.000046498, and discarded zero outputs; the unavailable direct DeepSeek request returned before token billing.

### Stage 13: Colombia reasoning timeout and invalid Pro escalation repair v2.0.4

CO Retail v2.0.3 accumulated USD 1.6755584861 across 66 events, accepted 466/486 raw outputs, used 296 downstream, and still published 0/50. Product cost was USD 1.4990933861; Gemini cost was USD 0.1764651000. Summed event latency was 1,025,291 ms with four retries. Discards included 117 duplicates, 78 wrong-primary-role/gate outcomes, 52 reroutes, 17 light-gate rejects, 10 provider-cooldown skips, and 17 explicit provider/rate-limit/circuit/HTTP failures. No USD 10/20/30 checkpoint was crossed; the forecast was USD 14.7022 expected and USD 17.1652 upper.

The dominant defect was output utilization, not acquisition scarcity: the cache held 51 corrected candidates, but 47 were deterministic unresolved fallbacks after reasoning-enabled routine calls timed out. Observable late-round model events generated 21,795 reasoning tokens that were not required for compact classification. Generic catch handlers then upgraded infrastructure failures to Pro, producing paid work without the required semantic evidence of an eight-point or critical-state change.

v2.0.4 disables optional reasoning for automatic public OpenRouter DeepSeek requests, shares one provider circuit across a Product cell, allows one same-tier single-candidate schema repair, and forbids Pro escalation for transport, timeout, balance or provider errors. A real cached-packet diagnostic produced one valid/used correction from one input, with 9,187 input tokens, 1,859 output tokens, zero reasoning tokens, 17,867 ms latency, no retry/discard and USD 0.0018067 account cost. This diagnostic is implementation evidence and is excluded from the formal experiment ledger. The formal resume must reuse all same-run search/evidence artifacts, measure corrected-to-in-role-to-scored utilization, and retain every prior sunk cost.

### Stage 14: Colombia token-aware correction and acquisition-closed recovery v2.0.5

The v2.0.4 recovery consumed 51 cached correction inputs but produced zero model-valid corrections and zero downstream-used outputs. Large multi-candidate dossiers caused the first OpenRouter calls to exceed 75 seconds; subsequent requests were skipped after those request-local timeouts opened the fallback circuit. The recovery-boundary defect then launched discovery round five. That round added USD 0.368 to the formal ledger—USD 0.080 discovery, USD 0.168 fresh evidence and USD 0.120 correction evidence—produced 16 corrected records, and used zero for scoring/final output. Cumulative spend is USD 2.0435738961; expected/upper completion forecasts are USD 16.1742/USD 19.1782; no mandatory checkpoint has been crossed.

v2.0.5 caps correction packets at ten relevance-prioritized current evidence items and 1,200 characters per excerpt, caps serialized batch input at 28,000 characters, and uses batch size one with concurrency two for same-run formal recovery. Request-scoped timeouts no longer trip the provider-wide circuit. Recovery is acquisition-closed, so its allowed outputs are only corrected roles, score-only assessments and final rankings from the 67 cached candidates. Regression verification covers schema repair, infrastructure non-escalation, timeout circuit behavior and recovery config; actual cached-recovery token, latency, valid-output and downstream-use efficiency will be recorded by the next run.

### Stage 15: Colombia localization, identity and concrete-primary repair v2.0.6

v2.0.5 correction consumed 67 inputs and returned 66 outputs, 57 model-valid corrections and 15 in-role outputs. Flash used 240,342 input/124,140 output tokens; two policy-qualified Pro calls used 6,790/2,335; reasoning was zero. Correction account cost was USD 0.0630092. Qualification consumed 15, returned 15, completed 12 and retained 6; Flash used 104,016/21,188 tokens and one Pro call used 7,585/1,691, for USD 0.0345714. Three assessments timed out. Total experiment cost reached USD 2.1411647, with USD 16.5645 expected and USD 19.7119 upper forecast and no budget checkpoint.

Only 6/50 final outputs exposed four upstream defects: Mexico city focuses polluted every Spanish Colombia query; Exa-owned profile URLs merged unrelated companies; incomplete Latin American public suffixes yielded invalid identity domains; and 19 Hybrid primaries included single-family Retailer+E-tailer cases. v2.0.6 keys localization by country code, isolates Exa provenance from identity, normalizes observed public suffixes, and converts same-family Hybrid to a concrete role. The formal search extension reuses every prior valid score/evidence, retries only incomplete cached assessments, excludes cached domains and measures new discovery-to-gate-to-evidence-to-role-to-score utilization separately. SearchAPI's exhausted quota remains an explicit provider failure rather than silently changing the arm.

The formal repair entry point accepts only a completed zero-output Product outage artifact. It reuses the same-run intent, RAG, playbook, discovery results, fresh evidence and correction-stage supplemental evidence; only semantic correction/scoring and still-untried discovery rounds may execute. This prevents 27 candidates' already acquired evidence from being purchased again and records the reuse as a zero-cost cache event. Six focused files currently pass 41 regression tests and typecheck passes; implementation-test external cost is otherwise zero. Remaining optimization opportunities are to persist provider quota/circuit health across cells, include minimal schema-valid provider calls in formal preflight, and quantify fallback utilization/cost after recovery rather than removing SearchAPI from one quota incident.

### Stage 16: Colombia evidence affiliation and target-country gate repair v2.0.7

The v2.0.6 Retail extension reached 11/50 at cumulative USD 3.2347474982 but its valid-output count was not trustworthy. Earlier suffix collapse had turned `com.pe` and `co.cr` into evidence targets, so unrelated national-domain pages were marked official. Correction search also retained pages that did not name the candidate, and qualification could independently override country evidence. Exa profiles/domainless IDs inflated discovery yield, one overlong gate reason held a whole batch, and incomplete model rounds advanced exhaustion. These are product defects rather than evidence scarcity.

v2.0.7 requires a registrable domain before paid official evidence, filters correction results by exact domain, normalized candidate name, distinctive domain label or multi-token entity match, and instructs the correction model to answer country presence only for the requested market. Qualification deterministically downgrades or rejects a target-country gate not supported by the correction finding. Discovery-gate output is schema-bounded without inventing positive facts; provider profiles and domainless identities are discarded from valid/new-company counts; model-failed rounds do not advance exhaustion; and an explicit 50-company cell receives up to ten bounded rounds.

The corrupted Product artifact will be archived and excluded from outcome metrics, while all prior cost stays in the budget ledger and the frozen Gemini result is reused. This cache invalidation is versioned: reuse is mandatory for overlapping valid work, but artifacts proven to violate identity/evidence lineage cannot be reused. Implementation inputs were four invalid-lineage examples plus the 96-record cached pool; valid outputs are the affiliation/country guard, bounded gate normalization, corrected telemetry, exhaustion policy and restart boundary. Six focused test files consume these outputs. External implementation calls, tokens, search credits, latency and retries are 0; actual restart efficiency remains pending. Optimization opportunities to measure are affiliation-filter discard rate, gate-schema salvage rate, country-gate downgrade rate, cost per final candidate, and whether SearchAPI's persistent quota state should be cached across cells.

### Stage 17: Colombia Retail clean restart and incomplete-output diagnosis

The v2.0.7 clean Product restart published 6/50 requested Retailer/E-tailer candidates while reusing the frozen 15/50 Gemini control. Cumulative formal cost is USD 5.2302219218 across 260 events: Gemini USD 0.1764651 and Product USD 5.0537568218, including all invalidated/debug versions as sunk cost. The one-cell forecast is USD 28.9207 expected and USD 36.6090 upper against the USD 50 hard cap. Spend is 10.46%, so no mandatory USD 10/20/30 review has yet been crossed.

The clean restart consumed 1,027 aggregate inputs, emitted 2,557 raw outputs, retained 2,083 valid outputs and used 1,056 downstream. Aggregate valid-output rate is 81.46% and downstream utilization is 50.70%, but those totals include repeated search rounds and cache stages and must not be interpreted as candidate precision. Discovery found fresh domain-bearing candidates during five rounds; later zero-yield rounds did not buy correction evidence. The final six scores are 68, 68, 68, 62, 57 and 53. Evidence affiliation and target-country binding remained intact in the reviewed output.

The binding underfill is model availability, not demonstrated market scarcity. Among 47 correction records, 27 are unresolved and 21 of those are deterministic fallbacks after the direct DeepSeek endpoint reported insufficient balance and the OpenRouter DeepSeek route timed out. Three more assessments are retry-required. Round one spent correction-search credits for 29 candidates but produced only 13 model-valid corrections and four requested-family corrections; all seven round-five correction inputs fell back deterministically. SearchAPI's monthly quota also remained unavailable. Therefore the 6/50 output cannot enter a quality comparison until acquisition-closed semantic recovery is attempted.

The next implementation must give each fallback route its own bounded deadline, add a public-only peer-provider route at the same routine/escalation capability tier, and recover only deterministic corrections plus incomplete or materially affected scores. Search, first evidence and correction-search evidence are cache hits with zero repeated API credit. Each fallback event must retain requested and actual provider/model, tokens, account and official cost, latency, attempts, retries and discard reason. An automatic optimization opportunity is also retained: persist provider quota health across cells and conditionally replace an unavailable discovery source without concurrently duplicating equivalent search-engine work.

### Stage 18: bounded peer-provider fallback and incomplete-only recovery v2.0.8

Runtime v3.7.0 preserves direct DeepSeek as primary and permits at most two equivalent fallbacks for public packets: the same DeepSeek tier through OpenRouter and then an OpenAI peer tier through the same gateway. The routes have independent 45-second and 25-second deadlines inside the existing outer request budget. Failed-route attempts and retries are now included in successful fallback telemetry instead of reporting only the primary failure. Private workspace content still requires an explicitly approved private route; embeddings have no fallback.

The Colombia runner adds an acquisition-closed `--repair-incomplete` mode. It retries only correction records whose prior model is `deterministic-fallback`, then scores only records that are missing, retry-required or materially changed by that correction. All completed unaffected corrections and scores, discovery results, homepages, fresh evidence and supplemental evidence are reused. Ranking is rebuilt from the complete cache, so the existing six eligible Retail results cannot disappear merely because they were not reprocessed. Empty model-usage stages still emit an explicit failed efficiency event instead of becoming invisible.

The OpenAI peer choice follows the official structured-output capability and token price for `gpt-4o-mini`; `gpt-4o` is reserved for an already policy-qualified escalation rather than infrastructure failure. The first repository-only implementation verification incurs no new search/extraction credits. A prior minimal capability diagnostic used 131 input and 6 output tokens, 1.515 seconds and USD 0.00002325 account cash cost; it sent no candidate or private content and is not yet part of the formal ledger. The frozen provider check will repeat one minimal schema call after tagging and record its exact runtime/account cost before recovery. Optimization opportunities remain: measure peer-fallback adoption and cost per recovered correction, and do not make the peer primary unless quality and availability evidence—not one outage—support that change.

### Stage 19: Colombia Retail fallback measurement

Both v2.0.8 structured preflights passed. The same-tier OpenRouter DeepSeek call cost USD 0.0000080017 and the direct OpenAI peer call cost USD 0.0000237. Acquisition-closed recovery retried 21 deterministic corrections and nine missing, retry-required or materially affected scores. It raised the Product Retail output from 6 to 13, adding seven candidates for USD 0.0339371944: correction USD 0.0212534135 and scoring USD 0.0126837809. Search, homepage and Tavily credits were zero. The complete experiment reached USD 5.2641908179; expected/upper forecasts are USD 29.0565/USD 36.7947, and no USD 10/20/30 checkpoint was crossed.

Correction returned 21 records, 11 with resolved roles and six in the requested family. Qualification returned nine completed assessments. OpenRouter DeepSeek carried 34,711 input/22,035 output correction tokens and 48,432/10,836 scoring tokens; OpenAI `gpt-4o-mini` carried 40,359/6,223 and 17,372/2,178 respectively. The aggregate event model correctly retains failed-route attempts in the eventual fallback usage, but stage-level batch persistence delayed real-time token visibility until completion. Per-call or per-candidate checkpointing is therefore an explicit future observability optimization.

Review found two policy contradictions in otherwise schema-valid output. Mercado Libre's supported findings called it a third-party seller marketplace while the role remained E-tailer. Techniservice had `Unknown` scale but received 15/15 scale/coverage, and its 15/15 buying-influence score exceeded the existing 9/15 deterministic evidence cap. The 13-result state is retained as an observed but not final-quality artifact; the repair must not repeat any external call.

### Stage 20: deterministic role and score consistency v2.0.9

Runtime v3.8.0 removes Retail/E-tail roles only when supported semantic findings explicitly establish a third-party seller marketplace, caps unknown scale at a neutral 8/15 rather than zero or maximum, and enforces the existing buying-influence evidence cap before recomputing total, range, recommendation priority and account tier. The model remains responsible for semantic findings and raw sub-scores; code enforces internally consistent totals and states.

The Colombia `--repair-consistency` path reuses all model semantic output, discovery and evidence, applies the same deterministic rules to cached records, and rebuilds ranking without a model, search, homepage or Tavily call. Implementation input was the 47 cached corrections and their cached assessments; code/tests/docs are all downstream-used, discarded implementation output is 0, and external tokens, credits, cash cost, latency and retries are 0. Full verification passed 88 files/392 tests, typecheck passed and lint has 0 errors with 11 pre-existing warnings.

The measured zero-cost repair processed 66 cached correction/assessment records, removed one explicit marketplace from the requested family and capped 15 assessments. Product Retail changed from 13 to 12 outputs. Techniservice moved from 100 to 87, Alkosto from 88 to 78 and Homecenter from 80 to 70; the complete final score sequence is 87, 78, 70, 64, 64, 64, 64, 60, 58, 55, 54 and 51. Formal cost stayed USD 5.2641908179 and no checkpoint was crossed. The superseded 13-result state remains recoverable in Git history, and all prior cost remains visible.

### Stage 21: conditional SearchAPI provider-gap fallback v2.0.10

The consistency-normalized CO Retail arm remained 12/50 after eight cached rounds. SearchAPI accounted for 40 planned calls, of which five failed with the confirmed monthly-quota HTTP 429 and 35 were skipped by the provider circuit/recovery cooldown; its raw, normalized, unique and downstream-used output were all zero. Because national Retail and E-tail both depended on SearchAPI for broad Google-index recall, Brave, Exa and Places were complementary but could not replace the missing route. Repeating the unchanged plan would therefore consume latency without addressing the observed provider gap.

Hybrid-search v1.6.0 and runtime v3.9.0 add Gemini Full only as a conditional backup for configured SearchAPI-dependent Retail/E-tail and Reseller/VAR tracks. Eligibility requires an observed failed track, SearchAPI provider/invocation circuit or recovery cooldown. Healthy SearchAPI leaves the backup as a zero-call `fallback-provider-healthy` skip; ordinary low yield does not activate it. Gemini Product remains disabled and Tavily remains evidence-only. Shared identity deduplication and cached-domain exclusions apply to the fallback, and explicit `fallbackUsed` telemetry separates contingency contribution from routine provider contribution.

Implementation input was the 40 SearchAPI call records and 12 valid cached outputs. Valid implementation output is one provider-gap route primitive across five configured tracks plus schema validation, invocation/skip telemetry, tests and synchronized product/experiment documentation; all implementation output is downstream-used and no implementation artifact was discarded. External implementation calls, tokens, paid-search credits, cash cost, retries and external latency are zero. The frozen CO Retail repair will reuse all 12 valid outputs and compatible acquisition/semantic/score cache entries, so only new candidates may incur new cost. Runtime measurement must report fallback calls/tokens/cost/latency/retries, raw→unique→gate→correction→in-role→final yield, cached overlap and Tavily credits per incremental final candidate. Persisting task-wide provider quota health across separate cell processes remains an automatic optimization opportunity.

### Stage 22: Colombia provider-gap measurement, budget pause and cache repair v2.0.11

The real five-round provider-gap extension raised CO Retail from 12 to 14 final candidates for USD 4.0500391788, or USD 2.0250 per incremental final candidate. It processed 805 raw discovery results into 135 reported unique candidates, 81 light-gate/evidence/correction inputs, six in-role candidates and two final candidates: raw→final 0.25%, unique→final 1.48%, gate→in-role 7.41% and in-role→final 33.33%. Gemini Full ran 15 fallback calls with 132 grounding queries, returned 300 raw/149 normalized/49 unique candidates, cost USD 1.848 and first-discovered one final candidate (Ktronix). Google Places first-discovered the other final candidate (Multitintas) for USD 0.175. Brave cost USD 0.075 for 40 unique and no first-discovery final; Exa cost USD 0.333 for 32 unique and none; failed SearchAPI probes cost a conservative USD 0.032. New Tavily evidence/correction search cost USD 1.488 and model stages cost USD 0.0990391788.

The Product output is 14/50 and the experiment total is USD 9.4482299967. Although the mandatory USD 10 review has not yet been crossed, the runner paused because expected completion is USD 45.7926 and the conservative upper forecast is USD 59.6814, above the USD 50 cap. The forecast is deliberately conservative because all invalidated/debug Retail iterations are charged to the only completed cell and therefore extrapolated across three remaining cells. Paid work remains stopped pending the required budget decision.

The run also proved a cache-contract defect: 25/81 downstream candidate inputs (30.9%) had already appeared in prior discovered/enriched records. The extension code logged that cached domains were excluded but only added new corrected domains after each fresh round; it never preloaded the prior artifact. Prior correction/score records were not overwritten, so the 14 outputs remain usable, but repeated gate/evidence/correction work was unnecessary and its exact per-candidate cash allocation is not recoverable from aggregate events. Runtime v3.9.1 now preloads unique domains from prior discovered, rejected, enriched and corrected records before search, filtering matches before any paid downstream stage. Regression tests cover corrected-domain aliases and all three raw candidate collections. Remaining non-blocking optimization opportunities are to coalesce three same-round Gemini Full fallback tracks into one structured category-level request and to improve pre-evidence Retail action/networking precision; those change search quality/behavior and are not applied mid-experiment without a new frozen decision.

### Stage 23: user-confirmed budget resume control v2.0.12

The user confirmed the recommended response to the USD 59.6814 conservative forecast: freeze CO Retail at 14/50, perform no further Retail search, continue the remaining frozen categories, preserve the USD 50 hard cap and report USD 10/20/30 reviews. No Product search, evidence, model, scoring or metric rule changes in this stage.

The formal runner now persists a structured budget-forecast authorization containing confirmation time, reason, baseline spent/expected/upper values and a single remaining cell start. It permits only the next frozen `CO-distribution` cell to pass the already acknowledged forecast warning. Checkpoint events remain live during the cell; hard-stop and unpriced-cost conditions can never be bypassed. Forecast risk is evaluated again after the cell, avoiding both silent unlimited authorization and loss of a partially completed in-memory cell at the USD 10 boundary. Implementation input/output is one user decision to one persisted authorization and public audit artifact; external calls, tokens, credits, cash cost, retries and latency are zero.

### Stage 24: Colombia Distributor/VAD result and USD 10 review

CO Distributor/VAD completed without a process failure but underfilled both arms: Gemini returned 20/50 and Product returned 13/50 after seven rounds with `confirmed-exhaustion`. Product converted 398 raw provider results into 75 new unique candidates, 52 light-gate candidates, 51 corrected records, 21 requested-family records and 13 eligible final outputs. Raw→final utilization was 3.27%, unique→final 17.33% and gate→final 25.00%. Correction primary roles were Distributor 19, VAD 1, Unresolved 25 and six other-role/Hybrid records; the 49.0% unresolved share is the main quality bottleneck, but it followed insufficient role evidence rather than a provider or schema outage.

The cell cost USD 2.3886083303: Gemini control USD 0.179985375 and Product approximately USD 2.208623. Product discovery cost USD 1.115, Tavily fresh/correction evidence USD 1.000 and all remaining intent/RAG/model/ranking stages approximately USD 0.0936. Final first-discovery contribution was Gemini Full five, Brave five and Exa three. SearchAPI remained quota-unavailable with four failed probes, four retries and zero output. TD SYNNEX was correctly retained as a high-value Distributor with score 91; it was not assigned the downstream-only KA label.

The USD 10 checkpoint fired at USD 10.1746644570 during the cell and recorded no unpriced events or hard-stop condition. After the cell, cumulative cost is USD 11.8368383270, or 23.67% of budget. With two completed categories, the forecast contracted from USD 45.7926 expected/USD 59.6814 upper to USD 31.6736 expected/USD 45.5102 upper; budget risk is therefore back within the USD 50 boundary and execution may continue automatically. No code or frozen search/scoring rule changes are justified by this result alone. Product opportunities retained for later study are evidence-efficient resolution of role findings and the marginal value of rounds six/seven, which added no final candidate.

### Stage 25: Colombia SI/MSP result and saturation/recovery findings

CO SI/MSP completed at 15/50 Gemini-control and 5/50 Product candidates. Product converted 774 raw results into 154 new unique candidates, 121 corrected records, 16 completed assessments and five eligible SI outputs. Raw-to-final utilization was 0.65%, unique-to-final 3.25% and corrected-to-final 4.13%. Final additions by round were 2, 0, 2, 0, 0, 1, 0, 0, 0 and 0. The correction distribution was 53 Unresolved, 36 Hybrid, 15 SI, five Installer, five Brand Owner and seven other-role records, making role-resolution precision—not raw acquisition volume—the main bottleneck.

Provider contribution was uneven. Gemini Full produced 200 raw/111 normalized/72 new unique candidates for USD 0.952 and first-discovered two finals. Brave produced 400/383/54 for USD 0.100 and one final. Exa produced 160/31/25 for USD 0.296 and two finals. Places produced 14/12/3 for USD 0.350 and no final. SearchAPI made six failed probes and 24 skips, produced nothing and carried USD 0.048 conservative cost. The cell cost USD 4.3320164745 including the USD 0.12052675 control. Cumulative cost is USD 16.1688548015; expected/upper completion forecasts are USD 29.5584/USD 33.9922, so the USD 50 hard cap is not forecast at risk and the USD 20 checkpoint has not fired.

The run exposed two product opportunities. A detached terminal left the real Node child running without observable console output; a mistakenly launched duplicate was stopped after about 25 seconds. The frozen result and persisted event ledger contain only the original Product artifact, although one duplicate Kimi request may have reached the provider and cannot be reconstructed exactly. Long cells therefore need atomic stage/round snapshots, a run lease and explicit heartbeat so restart cannot race or lose an already purchased evidence packet. Separately, a persistent SearchAPI circuit counted as a fresh provider failure for every saturation decision. Rounds seven through ten added no final candidate and the last two rounds added only one new unique candidate, yet the circuit forced execution to the tenth round. After the experiment, confirmed persistent outages with healthy alternate-route coverage should remain visible in telemetry but should not reset no-final exhaustion; this must be regression-tested before changing production behavior.

### Stage 26: Colombia Reseller/VAR result and USD 20 review

CO Reseller/VAR completed at 10/50 Gemini-control and 7/50 Product candidates. Product converted 939 raw results into 152 new unique candidates, 97 corrected records, 10 completed assessments and seven eligible outputs. Raw-to-final utilization was 0.75%, unique-to-final 4.61% and corrected-to-final 7.22%. Final additions by round were 1, 0, 1, 2, 1, 0, 0, 2, 0 and 0. Role correction produced 71 Unresolved, eight Distributor, eight VAR, five SI, two Reseller, two Retailer and one Hybrid, so 73.2% unresolved role output is the dominant bottleneck.

The cell cost USD 5.0794309001 including the USD 0.233923925 control. Product discovery cost USD 2.880: Gemini Full USD 2.128 for 400 raw/193 normalized/82 new unique candidates and five first-discovered finals; Places USD 0.350 for 35/35/11 and no final; Exa USD 0.259 for 140/30/26 and no final; Brave USD 0.095 for 364/355/33 and two finals; failed SearchAPI probes USD 0.048 with zero output. Although Gemini fallback was the most expensive source, it cannot simply be removed because it sourced five of seven final outputs. The next optimization should coalesce its two fallback tracks and gate later calls on observed marginal final yield.

The USD 20 checkpoint fired at USD 20.0346226322. Expected/upper completion forecasts were USD 33.4242/USD 38.4378 and there were no unpriced events or hard-stop conditions. After all four search cells, cumulative cost is USD 21.2482857016, or 42.50% of budget; the evaluation-inclusive expected/upper forecast is USD 30.2483/USD 34.7855. The cell ended at ten rounds with `maximum-rounds`; rounds six/seven/nine/ten added no final, while round eight added two, so a simple fixed earlier round cap would have lost value. Saturation control therefore needs outage-aware marginal-yield logic rather than an unconditional round reduction.

### Stage 27: Colombia blind-judge parameter compatibility v2.0.13

All four shared control-evaluation cells completed and are cached. Blind audit then failed before inference for two concurrent GPT-5.6-sol Judge-B packets: OpenRouter returned HTTP 404 `Filter by Parameters`, with zero input/output tokens and USD 0 cost. The request combined high reasoning, strict JSON Schema, `require_parameters: true` and `temperature: 0`. Temperature was not part of the judging design and is unnecessary for a reasoning model; retaining it removed every otherwise eligible provider endpoint.

v2.0.13 removes only `temperature` from high-reasoning blind-review requests. It preserves GPT-5.6-sol, Claude Opus 5, strict structured output, provider/data policy, blind evidence, dual-family consensus, conditional arbitration and all gates. A focused regression test captures the OpenRouter body and proves that high reasoning/schema enforcement remain while temperature is absent. The two failed calls stay in the evaluation ledger; completed shared evaluation and valid per-judge cache entries are reused. This is a provider-compatibility repair, not outcome-guided prompt or scoring tuning.

### Stage 28: Colombia blind-output bounded normalization v2.0.14

After v2.0.13 restored GPT-5.6-sol routing, four shared-evaluation cells completed and the blind audit reached paid inference. One Claude result was discarded because a single dimension reason exceeded the frozen 500-character maximum, despite strict JSON Schema being requested. At interruption the experiment had spent USD 22.4916165466: two valid GPT decisions cost USD 0.045892, one additional GPT decision USD 0.018591, one valid Claude decision USD 0.074965, and two schema-invalid Claude outputs USD 0.169690. The valid per-judge decisions are cached; no completed consensus packet had yet been recorded.

v2.0.14 applies deterministic schema-bound clipping to judge narrative strings and arrays before validation. It cannot alter scores, roles, identity/market/eligibility states, citation support labels or evidence packets. Invalid/missing semantic fields continue to fail. Regression coverage verifies every bounded narrative collection and proves numerical dimensions remain byte-for-byte unchanged. This converts avoidable paid-output discard into usable structured output while preserving the blind rubric and gate.

### Stage 29: Colombia same-tier arbitrator redundancy v2.0.15

Bounded normalization allowed valid Claude/GPT decisions to reach consensus processing. Two packets required arbitration, but the DeepSeek Pro direct endpoint returned an unavailable degraded state and both zero-token/zero-cost arbitrator events failed. The experiment total reached USD 22.6660815466. No consensus packet was marked complete, while all valid judge decisions remained in the per-decision cache.

v2.0.15 applies the product's confirmed model-redundancy contract to arbitration: DeepSeek Pro remains primary and a missing or invalid direct result receives one bounded same-tier request through OpenRouter. Combined telemetry reports the original requested model, gateway actual model/provider, summed attempts/retries/latency and gateway account cost; no different judge family silently substitutes. A focused test proves two direct attempts followed by exactly one `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro` gateway request. If that route also fails, the run retains an explicit degraded state for the separately authorized in-conversation Codex fallback.

### Stage 30: Colombia bounded arbitrator schema repair v2.0.16

The same-tier gateway restored DeepSeek Pro inference, but two paid arbitrator responses ended at the 4,096-token ceiling and produced truncated JSON. At diagnosis the formal ledger contained 788 events and USD 22.8296226926 total budget cost; 17 blind events accounted for USD 0.6471441460. Four shared-evaluation cells were complete, no blind packet had yet reached a cached consensus, and the USD 10/USD 20 reviews remained satisfied without an unpriced event.

v2.0.16 allows exactly one repair only for an arbitrator response with paid/raw output and a local parse error. It reuses the identical packet, evidence, rubric, dimensions and requested role, routes directly to the already selected same-tier actual model, adds a concise-complete-JSON instruction, and raises only that retry's output ceiling to 8,192 tokens. The invalid call and repair use separate cost events so input/output tokens, cash cost, latency, discarded schema output and downstream utilization remain auditable. Request/transport failures, valid outputs and semantic-validation failures cannot trigger the repair; a second malformed result still stops for the authorized in-conversation Codex fallback. Focused tests verify the model stays `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro`, the enlarged budget is repair-only and the request carries the bounded repair instruction. This avoids repeating direct-provider attempts and avoids discarding a second full judgment for a formatting defect without changing any experiment outcome rule.

### Stage 31: Auditable in-conversation Codex arbitration fallback v2.0.17

Both v2.0.16 repair calls again returned invalid JSON at the 8,192-token ceiling. Together they cost USD 0.0525989 and produced zero downstream-used outputs; cumulative experiment cost reached USD 22.9137556926 across 792 paid/runtime events plus two zero-cost in-session events. Continuing the same DeepSeek route would repeat a demonstrated failure mode, so the preregistered and user-authorized `codex-in-session` fallback is activated without another external model call.

v2.0.17 adds a controlled importer instead of permitting opaque cache edits. One structured input becomes one schema-validated arbitration decision, one judge-bound cache entry, one public provenance artifact and one zero-token/zero-API-cost ledger event. Import fails unless the packet belongs to the frozen sample, both different-family judge decisions exist, deterministic rules require arbitration, blindness flags are false, citations reference packet evidence and the result resolves consensus. Two current Retail packets were resolved from the same evidence and judge outputs: NEXSA at 73/100 for a nine-point judge gap and Tecno Nacho at 66/100 for a score-threshold disagreement. No external knowledge or search was used. This stage exposes an optimization opportunity: future formal runners should implement the configured fallback ingestion path before execution and open a provider/output circuit after bounded same-route schema failure, rather than repurchasing the same malformed output.

### Stage 32: Preserve requested-model identity across schema repair v2.0.18

The resumed blind audit reached USD 23.6622914571. One DeepSeek repair succeeded and another failed after the bounded retry, proving the route is intermittent rather than universally unavailable. Inspection then found that successful repairs cached the slash-qualified gateway route as `requestedModel`, although the cache key and frozen experiment request use `deepseek-v4-pro`. This would discard valid downstream-used output during resumption and repurchase identical work.

v2.0.18 preserves the pre-repair requested model while retaining the gateway route in `actualModel`. Two already valid repair caches are metadata-corrected in place without changing semantic output, score, role, evidence, tokens, cash cost or raw response. The failed Homecenter arbitration is instead completed by the audited in-session fallback at 70/100, using only the frozen packet and two judge decisions, with zero API cost. Inputs and outputs are one code-level identity correction to two reused cache entries plus one schema-validated manual decision; all three become downstream-usable and no model/search call is repeated. The concrete long-term optimization is to separate logical model identity, provider route and actual response model as distinct mandatory fields in every reusable workflow cache.

### Stage 33: Response-body timeout normalization and conservative metering v2.0.19

At USD 24.7877959034, a DeepSeek schema-repair request reached the 180-second deadline while reading the response body. `fetch` had already returned headers, so the timeout occurred outside the retry catch, terminated the evaluation process and emitted neither usage nor cost. This is a workflow reliability and cost-observability defect: a possibly billed request cannot disappear from the ledger or be represented as zero.

v2.0.19 encloses both header acquisition and body reading in the bounded retry handler. A final timeout now becomes a failed structured provider call; it cannot crash the runner. When the response body provides no usage receipt, input is conservatively estimated from rubric plus serialized packet/judges at three characters per token and output is reserved at the configured ceiling for every attempt. Official list pricing is used and labeled `official-conservative`; account cash cost remains explicitly unavailable. A regression test simulates body-read `TimeoutError`, proves exactly two calls, one retry, timeout classification and aggregate conservative usage. The escaped Help Soluciones repair is backfilled at USD 0.009239835 official-conservative cost and then resolved by the audited in-session reviewer at 68/100 with zero API cost. Cumulative budget cost is USD 24.7970357384. This stage converts one untracked input into a failed-but-priced event and one downstream-used fallback, while retaining the raw uncertainty for final reporting.

### Stage 34: Ledger-aware schema repair for every blind reviewer v2.0.20

At USD 25.4363436899, Claude Opus 5 returned malformed JSON for one independent judge packet after consuming USD 0.1285. The external model was available and returned content, so this qualifies for one same-model repair rather than a different-family replacement. The prior implementation offered this recovery only to arbitrators. It also restarted every uncached decision at its initial call even when the cost ledger already proved that exact first call had failed, creating avoidable repeat spend after any process interruption.

v2.0.20 gives independent judges and arbitrators the same one-repair contract and reads the live cost ledger before authorization. One prior initial `schemaInvalid` event maps to a direct 8,192-token repair; one prior repair failure maps to the already authorized in-session fallback with no new provider request; a valid cache still short-circuits both. Repair events are separately identified, preserve the logical requested model and record the actual gateway route. Input is the existing packet plus failure state; output is either one cached valid decision or one explicit fallback requirement. No evidence or semantic result changes. The expected efficiency gain is one avoided initial model call per resumed malformed decision, with its exact realized savings measured in subsequent events.

### Stage 35: Blind family-label normalization v2.0.21

The formal workflow finished at USD 27.1095456691 with a positive 9.80 Macro Slot Utility@50 delta and Product wins in all four cells, but the overall gate failed because reported blind role-family agreement was 16.7%. Audit found that the two independent judges agreed on role family for 100% of representative packets and consistently used the rubric's canonical family labels, while the evaluator accepted only concrete role subtypes. Thus `retail`, `distribution`, `services` and `resale` were all deterministically mapped to `unresolved`; the apparent calibration failure was partly a taxonomy parser defect.

v2.0.21 accepts exact canonical family keys before subtype lookup and recomputes cached decision family/match fields against each packet on read. A 17-case regression matrix covers every family name, representative subtype, Hybrid and an unknown label. The completed run can regenerate derived calibration and final reports from the 64 cached independent judgments and 27 cached arbitrations without any API/search/model call or cost change. Inputs are cached semantic outputs; outputs are corrected family mappings and downstream metrics. Score correlations, MAE, citations and raw outputs are intentionally unchanged, so any remaining failed gates after recomputation represent substantive scoring/role disagreement rather than this parser error.

### Stage 36: Immutable-output arbitration cache identity v2.0.22

The first zero-cost v2.0.21 recomputation was stopped before any new cost event when corrected judge family fields changed every arbitration cache key. The legacy key incorrectly included `roleFamily` and `requestedCategoryFamilyMatch`, which are derived interpretations of the already hashed model output. This made a parser correction look like a semantic-input change and would have repurchased valid arbitration.

v2.0.22 removes those derived fields from the key, retaining protocol, full packet, judge identity, actual model and complete judge output. The deterministic migration read 92 existing files: 64 judge entries and 28 legacy arbitrator cache files representing 27 packet decisions, including one superseded duplicate. It wrote 27 new arbitration keys while preserving legacy files for audit. It also normalized cached derived fields and recorded aggregate input/output/usage, zero tokens, zero API cost and latency as a formal migration event. A regression test proves that changing only derived role-family fields cannot alter the key. No external call, score, evidence or prior cost event changed during migration. The resulting cache prevents the same class of cost leak in later taxonomy-parser revisions.

### Stage 37: Colombia final outcome, cost and optimization record

The corrected result is a search-outcome win but formal E2E no-pass. Product Macro Slot Utility@50 is 13.08 versus Gemini 3.28, delta +9.80; Product wins 4/4 cells and the bootstrap 95% lower bound is +6.31. Product has 38 evaluated-valid companies versus Gemini's ten but returns only 39/200 requested slots. The blind gate fails at 75.0% role agreement, 66.7% qualification agreement, 0.285 within-cell macro Spearman and 11.54 MAE. Citation alignment (100%) and entailment (94.1%) pass. Near-zero mean bias (-0.96) with high MAE shows company-level variance rather than directional inflation.

Final budget cost is USD 27.1385444613 across 901 events, 54.3% of the USD 50 cap: Product USD 20.5373845516, evaluation USD 5.8902587597 and Gemini USD 0.7109011500. Reviews at USD 10 and USD 20 passed; completion preceded USD 30. Cost is USD 0.067846/requested slot, USD 0.274127/returned candidate and USD 0.565386/evaluated-valid candidate. Hybrid discovery, first evidence and correction evidence total USD 19.710, or 96.0% of Product cost, so the next target is pre-evidence role precision/provider activation rather than model downgrading. Product wall time is 180.78 minutes versus 1.33 for control.

Discovery produced Brave 206 new unique/16.5 final credits for USD 0.550, Gemini Full 226/12.5 for USD 5.754, Exa 142/5 for USD 2.117, Places 45/5 for USD 1.505 and SearchAPI 0/0 for USD 0.288. Blind waste was five invalid judge and 32 invalid arbitrator events costing about USD 0.8144. The v2.0.22 recomputation unexpectedly required one missing DeepSeek arbitration because the legacy key depended on the corrected derived role; its initial+repair USD 0.028999 remains disclosed. The final analysis records category-specific route decisions, role/score calibration, provider-health caching, outage-aware saturation, cross-track deduplication and evidence-budget controls. No semantic production score change is made from this single market before a frozen-evidence calibration test.
